## 2026-11-01 - POST QA v0.2 GM ECONOMY ADMISSION: #010 PARTIAL ROOT-CAUSE REPAIR

**QA disposition unchanged: 7/17 source-fixed, eight partial (#010 included), two evidence watches.** No new QA installer or ZIP was made, Nestor has NOT received v0.2 according to the user, and the existing portable v0.2 executable predates this change and the prior post-v0.2 #003 source correction.

**Confirmed native gap:** In GM world-intervention/exact-event authoring, the prompt already required a concrete economicAccounting outcome for every Economy-tagged event, but the native Preview/Apply preflight did not enforce it. A model could author an Economy narrative without an accounting declaration or claim status applied with only projects/prose. GM UI warned that an effect was not actually booked, but the transaction could still look approved. The separate Timeline generated-answer economics validator is intentionally stronger and its custom-stat semantics were not rewritten.

**Surgical repair:** src/Game/AI/gameMasterValidation.js now exports the pure validateGameMasterEconomicEventAccounting. In event modes, Economy-tagged events must supply a valid applied/unquantified/none outcome with concrete 12..240-character reason and validated exposure provenance; an applied claim needs a measurable typed statsEffects operation; a national GDP, growth or debt effect may not be misdeclared unquantified/none even if tagged Disaster or Politics. A legitimately unquantified consequence is not assigned invented GDP. Independent author-defined custom effects remain legal; GM Preview still discloses that they alone do not settle national macro effects. Political events without economic claims and explicit Direct administration retain original authority. The existing GM validateGameMasterPreviewPayload now calls the guard before Preview and the Apply path reruns that same preflight, allowing the native corrective model retry to identify the exact event path. No GDP or monetary simulation changed.

**Regression evidence:** Three tests introduced red before implementation in src/Game/AI/gameMasterValidation.test.js (missing export), now green. The economic GM + generated Timeline targeted subset passed 44/44. ## 2026-11-01 - POST QA V0.2: SCENARIO STATS DEFINITION CONTENT GUARD (UNCOMMITTED)

**Finding #003: still PARTIAL; original score unchanged at 7/17 source-fixed, eight partial, two evidence watches.** This source checkpoint is AFTER the already packaged QA v0.2. The original portable zip and 73-file v0.2 source overlay were not rewritten. Same isolated Beta / Statistics V2 worktree and unchanged baseline HEAD b1c820c6755b820c8e4aad0f4876bc8f341e5598. No Git reset, stash, commit, stage, merge, push or real game/save modification.

**Actual confirmed root cause:** both server/libraryStore.js and src/runtime/web/libraryStore.js implement scenario assetStatus.stats as Boolean file presence, not the content/version of stats.json. The existing preview/save path checked that presence and therefore failed to detect a different same-present Stats definition (true->true) in a concurrently edited scenario. A different definition can change metric keys, bounds and explicit nativeMetric bindings without changing the saved countryStats before Apply. Our initial diagnosis had incorrectly claimed NO Stats asset check; closer inspection disproved this and exploratory edits were restored byte-exact. This new patch addresses the actual same-presence gap, not a fictional missing guard.

**Source changes:** One shared pure content guard assertScenarioStatsDefinitionContentsCurrent in src/runtime/scenarioStatsAuthoring.js uses existing normalized and serialized Stats definitions and fails closed on missing current contents. src/runtime/library.js adds optional fresh:true to downloadScenarioJsonAsset, using fetch cache:no-store without changing normal fetches. ScenarioStatsAuthoringPanel.jsx checks current actual stats.json before committing reviewed country baselines or explicitly approved legacy alias archives. libraryBar.jsx checks the same source asset before both dedicated definition-only Save (comparing last saved editorStatsSavedRef, NOT unsaved draft) and general Scenario Save (before its first world write). Both desktop and IndexedDB Boolean assetStatus contracts are preserved. No native GDP or customStats values are invented, moved or silently altered.

**Test evidence:** Red-before-green regressions in src/runtime/scenarioStatsAuthoring.test.js, src/Game/GameUI/scenarioStatsAuthoringWiring.test.js, src/Game/GameUI/statsNativeBindingUiWiring.test.js. Focused 62/62 passed. ## 2026-11-01 - QA FISCAL POLITY-RENAME INTEGRITY: SOURCE-FIXED SUBDEFECT (NO COMMIT)

**Original QA score remains 7/17 source-fixed requiring live acceptance, #009 PARTIAL.** The working tree is C:\dev\open-historia-beta-stats-integration-20261028, branch integrate/stats-v2-beta-2026-10-28, unchanged HEAD b1c820c6755b820c8e4aad0f4876bc8f341e5598. Do not equate narrow native bug repairs with resolution of the full nominal-to-national fiscal transaction lifecycle.

**Verified source issue:** server/polityRename.js correctly re-keyed scenario-priced military expenditure commitments, but not the subsequently added canonical world.fiscalTransactions and world.fiscalTaxPolicies. Grant liabilities, actual previously budgeted payment receipts and nominal tax-foregone forecasts could remain attached to a renamed-away polity. Added 44-line regression to server/polityRename.test.js, red before fix. The shared rename now moves both fiscal owner references while preserving precise amounts/currencies, full accrued and paid histories, stable law/payment event IDs, same-country repeats and unrelated foreign polity rows. No broad key rewriting or changing the amounts.

**Verified Preview/Apply authority divergence:** A fiscal authorization for a country renamed in the SAME event was admitted by applyEventImpactsToWorld because the native reducer applies polityChanges first, but rejected in validateFiscalEventOperations before GM Preview. A new real-reducer regression was red before repair; the bounded fiscal preflight now projects only existing active polity renames using the SAME pure server/polityRename.js helper, refreshes the resolver after each event, and carries source and prior-event renames into read-only fiscal Preview rows. A second regression checks an earlier rename followed by a grant and paid tranche against the actual reducer, preserving source input. A third red-before-green test found that the first patch introduced a fiscal rename-clash rejection into ordinary event batches with no fiscal operations; an explicit no-fiscal-ops guard restores the former non-fiscal validation boundary.

**Monetary-accounting decision:** Country Stats formats the national GDP aggregate as 2026-EUR-equivalent and separately lists economy.currency as the domestic unit. Treating a USD grant as a GDP percentage by using the latter field would silently forge an FX conversion. Therefore new-debt-financed nominal payment settlement remains unsupported absent a dated, sourced 2026-EUR-equivalent valuation, publicDebt stock authority, and double-count protection against the existing 240-day statsFiscalClock. Existing typed debtFinancedOutlayPercentGdp and scenario-priced military expenditure owner remain unchanged. Do not make a $600m grant an immediate debt posting merely because the sum is mentioned in story/progress.

**Validation:** Fiscal rename and Preview/Apply focused tests 44/44 passed. Windows npm.cmd test exit 0, 8,410 total, 8,407 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped, 2 TODO. **Canonical fiscal system, first bounded slice:** src/runtime/fiscalTransactions.js now owns a first-class nominal record ledger world.fiscalTransactions. Events carry a strict typed impacts.fiscalOps list of authorize, pay or cancel operations. Authorize records enacted nominal grant/relief/procurement/transfer with explicit currency, amount, polity, dated event source, reason and optional canonical same-owner Project; it DOES NOT pay a grant, change GDP, tax revenue, public debt or annual deficit. Pay requires an independently evidenced already-budgeted transfer, and can settle partial tranches while preserving dated payment receipts; unsupported new-debt funding fails closed. Cancel closes remaining authority but preserves past receipts. Runtime rejects missing/foreign projects, absent polities, invalid amounts/currencies, overpayment, backdating, unknown identities, duplicate ops, malformed persisted ledger, and unauthorized replay. Replaying the original authorization after payment/cancellation preserves its descendants. New module never writes into countryStats or converts currency to GDP.

## 2026-10-31 - FOLLOW-UP QA SOURCE REPAIR: REQUIRED TYPED CUSTOM STATISTICS TOOL FIELDS (UNCOMMITTED)

**Why the previous 17-finding source closure was not 17/17 fixed.** A follow-up source audit of #002 and the original October 21 Statistics V2 scoped RFC located a remaining *engine contract* mismatch: the scenario authoring assembler required every independent `customStats` key as a finite numeric value, while the AI provider tool exposed only `countryNJson` opaque text and the two native typed fiscal scalars. This includes historically reported `customStats.publicDebt` failures. A similarly named unbound custom reading is **not** necessarily the same as native `economy.publicDebt` and must never be substituted or automatically bound. Earlier diagnostic-only repairs did not address this deeper missing custom-field provider contract.

**Narrow correction.** `buildScenarioStartingStatsTool(expected, {customKeys})` now uses the author's *actual independent custom keys* to generate a separate required shallow `countryNCustomStats` object of explicitly required finite numeric fields, each bounded by the **existing** custom authoring range `[-1e18,+1e18]`, for custom sheets containing **1 through 20** independent keys. The authoritative country slot and required native `countryNPublicDebt`/`countryNBudgetBalance` fields remain unchanged. `interpretScenarioStatsAiResponse` rejects missing/invalid/extra custom fields, duplicate `customStats` carried in country JSON, and invalid values per country, without destroying valid companion country proposals or guessing zeros. The Scenario Editor passes the same `customKeys` to tool schema, prompt contract and interpreter. It keeps the old JSON transport for unusually large **over-20-key** custom sheets, avoiding a too-deep/large Gemini tool declaration. A zero-independent-key all-native custom sheet generates no unnecessary custom fields. Custom keys are schema-validated; the provider `toGeminiSchema` conversion retains required nested object/field contracts. This is a native contract change, not a general prompt hack or numeric schema relaxation.

**Regression evidence.** Focused 70/70 tests passed: tool/transport conversion, identity-safe two-country typed custom sheets, preserved independent canonical vs custom public-debt readings, exact zero, absent/type/range/extra/duplicate refusal, valid companion proposals, large-sheet compatibility, empty custom sheet, actual map-backed `createReviewedScenarioStatsSheet` acceptance and continued rejection of omitted custom debt in the native assembler. **Still unverified:** original six provider outputs, real Gemini compliance for nested tool declarations, cost/latency, Electron save/reload and game-state acceptance. This is another *partial* #002 source fix; it does **not** make 17/17 fixed. **Source-proven native integrity vulnerability (related to OH-PLAYTEST-016, not proof of the original save's failure).** Inspected canonical `politicalActorOps` mutation, `gameState.applyEventImpactsToWorld`, `buildPoliticalActorLegacyStatsProjection`, `gameMasterValidation`, and the GM Preview/Apply preflight. A Political Actor is the canonical writer of national government/head-of-state; the old `countryStats.government`/`leader` fields are a compatibility **projection** maintained when Political Actor operations apply. However, GM Direct `countryStatPatches[].patch` and event `impacts.polityChanges[].stats` could write either legacy field independently even when a real Political Actor exists for that polity. A direct in-memory native-writer reproduction (no save) yielded `politicalActors.byPolity.Germany.leader.name = Incumbent` alongside `countryStats.Germany.leader = Different Leader` after a structured but unchecked event. This is a real dual-ledger fork, not merely a prose-only concern.

**Shared native fix plus early GM feedback.** Added the pure `legacyPoliticalStatsWriteError(world, polityKey, patch)` contract in the existing Political World authority module `src/runtime/politicalActors.js`. The generic `gameState.applyPolityAndTerritoryImpacts` event writer now **throws before applying** legacy `leader` or `government` patches when that polity already owns an initialized Political Actor. This is a fail-closed invariant for Timeline, GM and any other event producer, not just a prompt or GM-specific guard. Native mutations are attempted on a detached world; a rejected conflicting event must not be silently recorded as applied. A wrapper `validateGameMasterPoliticalStatsAuthority(candidate, world)` in `src/Game/AI/gameMasterValidation.js` calls that same contract from the **shared** GM Preview/Apply preflight, providing the exact JSON path for correction before a GM transaction can be approved. The only accepted political writer remains `politicalActorOps`, which projects its accepted government and head-of-state to existing Stats sheets. This neither creates new Political Actors, guesses officeholders, rewrites or deletes historical saves, nor blocks GDP, stability, military or strategic-stat mutations. Polities without initialized Political World retain legacy-only Stats authoring. Distinct registered same-stock-base polities (Russia versus Russian Federation) do not borrow one another's actors.

**Verified tests and build.** The original detached-world probe reproduced the source vulnerability **before** the guard (Political Actor `Incumbent` versus Stats `Different Leader`). The regression now asserts that the same attempted event **throws** at the common native writer, both for leader and government; accepted Political Actor replacement still updates its existing Stats projection, and legitimate legacy-only event edits still work. It also rejects GM Direct and event-embedded conflicting legacy fields including unchanged duplicate values, permits nonpolitical Stats corrections and protects distinct same-stock-base identities. Native `gameState.politicalActorOps.test.js` covers the authoritative projection. Focused GM/political native suite passed **34/34**, with no validation weakening. Fresh final Windows `npm.cmd test` **exited 0**: **8,357 total / 8,354 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped / 2 TODO** (log `C:\Users\kernel\Downloads\OpenHistoria-QA-Checkpoint-11-tests-20261031.log`). **Remaining truth boundary.** OH-PLAYTEST-016's one-year Horse Flood observed Obama/Trump/May/Macron names in narrative but supplied no actual Political Actor ledger. We therefore **cannot attribute that historical watch to this GM bug** or claim Timeline automatically updated Political World. **OH-PLAYTEST-001 corrected.** Created a small pure budget contract in `src/Game/GameUI/scenarioStatsGenerationBudget.js`: default **10** requests, explicit opt-in up to **100** requests per run (up to three country proposals per call), strict integer and range checks, and confirmation before any run that would actually spend more than ten calls. Example: 202 countries require 68 sequential calls. Regression coverage proves whole roster selection, invalid values, default, and no charge warning when few calls are actually needed. A model-failed ordinary order receives no claimed action ID and remains `planned/overdue` after a fallback, with truthful short title and explanation; a genuine native chat/deployment path preserves existing action ID semantics. The fallback summary distinguishes pending orders rather than asserting the AI executed them. Focused tests cover the real Horse Flood action lifecycle, chat/deployment exclusions, and oversized order titles. **Real Timeline fallback and save/reload validation remain outstanding.**

**OH-PLAYTEST-011 diplomatic status duplication.** GM transport previously required the model to provide both absolute relation `score` and a redundant `status`, although the native diplomatic director derives the latter deterministically. `gmRelationUpdateSchema.status` is now optional for new proposals; the canonical score, distinct-polity validation, native causal linkage and explicit contradictory-status rejection remain in force. The GM generation contract directs the model to omit `status` and gives the actual numeric band map. Tests prove a score-only GM proposal passes the real schema and native validation, that +65 derives `friendly`, +25 derives `cordial`, and an explicit contradictory `cautious` remains rejected. This improves the native output contract; **real-model Test 45 re-run is not yet verified**.

New focused regression in `gameMasterValidation.test.js` reproduces the exact real empty Direct response and validates positive and negative cases; 34 focused GM/Auto/Stats tests passed with zero failures. This is an **independent provider-discovered GM request-completeness bug**, not a Statistics V2 feature rework.

## 2026-10-28 - GAME MASTER AUTO MODE: BOUNDED ONE-CALL ROUTING

**Decision and scope:** The Game Master now defaults to **Auto** in the player-facing cheats panel, with the existing **Direct**, **Exact Event** and **World Intervention** choices still available. A freeform player request triggers the existing single `gameMaster` generation call; the model selects one of the three actual modes in its existing structured `mode` field. **Auto is not a fourth canonical mutation mode** and causes no extra model-routing request. The model may provide a bounded optional `modeReason`; the real preview displays `Auto → chosen mode` and the rationale before Apply. Manual selection remains authoritative; switching mode or editing the request invalidates the previous preview.

**Native scope/authorization:** `src/Game/AI/gameMasterAutoMode.js` holds the one-call selected-mode admission rule. In Auto the model may choose Exact Event or World Intervention freely, but can choose **Direct** only for a conservatively recognizable, explicitly targeted baseline/administrative correction in the user request (e.g. *set Germany's GDP to a specified figure*). The Direct guard requires a correction verb, a named canonical field, and its actual replacement value in the **same short clause**; numeric fields additionally require a numeric target. An incidental occurrence of words such as *set*, *GDP* and *to* in a crisis narrative is not authority. World-process requests such as a banking panic cannot gain Direct authority merely because the model returns that field. An unsupported or ambiguous Auto-Direct selection **fails closed and triggers the existing GM validation/retry flow**; the user can explicitly select Direct for an intentional correction, including non-English requests not recognized by this conservative English-language gate. All three ordinary native validators, source/target bounds, schema checks, and the previously implemented GM intervention GDP rebase restriction stay active.

**Implementation continuity:** `previewGameMasterCommand` accepts `auto` only as a requested input preference; its payload must resolve to one of the three real modes *before* the existing preview validator runs. The preview persists both `requestedMode` and the authoritative `mode`; its transaction uses the latter. `applyGameMasterPreview` still accepts **only the three native modes**, rechecks the routing decision, validates the entire preview against fresh canonical state, and never invokes another model. The shallow Gemini-compatible tool transport and internal GM schema accept the optional rationale without changing required legacy transport fields; older saved/manual GM previews remain compatible. The two-column picker fits four choices on desktop, with single-column choices on mobile. `gameMasterAutoMode.test.js` covers the model-choice admission contract, unsafe Direct routes, explicit corrections, preserved manual authority, optional explanation decoding, and preview/Apply plumbing.

**Verified acceptance:** Focused GM routing, native validation, request-completeness, transport, and custom Stats tests passed **36/36**; targeted ESLint exit **0** (preexisting warnings in unchanged portions of the large gameplay module). The initial full-suite run identified exactly two integration expectations: the strict GM transport-property list lacked the new optional modeReason and the English i18n catalog lacked 11 new Auto UI strings. Both were corrected in their canonical contract/catalog files without weakening checks, dropping existing entries, or running mass translation regeneration. The confirmed final `npm.cmd test` run exited **0: 8,213 total / 8,210 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped / 2 TODO**. Production `npm.cmd run build` desktop and `npm.cmd run build:web` both exited **0**; the web build checked six map data assets (66.1 MB). A normal, **visible Chrome** browser (not headless), isolated profile, and loopback-only QA game server loaded the built UI; Game Menu -> Cheats -> GM Console displayed all four options with Auto visually selected by default, and actual clicks switched Direct then returned to Auto, updating the active state and examples. No provider call or GM Apply was made in that browser session. New source strings have been added to the canonical English catalog; newly untranslated UI strings may remain English in shipped language packs until separately translated, without charging the player AI requests for pack-backed interface. **Live Gemini Auto-selected transaction quality remains a user playtest gate**, not falsely claimed as verified. The corrupted 1912 banking save remains untouched; recover from the separate pre-GM export. This is one bounded GM reliability/UI milestone, **not** a renewed Statistics V2 scope expansion. External canons should be reconciled at the next Beta integration checkpoint, preserving prior unresolved debt.

## 2026-10-28 - POST-FREEZE LIVE GM CATASTROPHE: UNAUTHORIZED GDP BASELINE REBASE

**Repro, real provider and exported campaign:** The user submitted a Game Master `world-intervention` on **1912-03-31**, requesting a Berlin bank collapse and an ongoing, causally modeled financial crisis, explicitly saying estimates should be bounded and not fabricated measured losses. Genuine `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` returned an accepted `submit_game_master` transaction with an appropriate crisis timeline event and persistent `german-banking-crisis-1912` storyline, **plus an unauthorized** `countryStatPatches[0]` `German Empire` absolute `economy.gdp = 515000000000` and `stability = 62`. GM Apply did **not** advance the turn (round remained 2 and date 1912-03-31), but `applyCountryStatPatchToWorld` accepted the new whole-polity GDP baseline, reweighted the existing territorial components, and produced **EUR 514,999,999,971**, versus the pre-GM export's **EUR 168,941,993,728**: a **EUR 346,058,006,243 erroneous increase** despite the banking crisis. Stability fell from 75 to 62. The GM audit contains a matching `stats:0:German Empire` accepted operation; event and crisis remain canonical. Both the 1912 pre-GM and post-GM user exports are preserved as immutable evidence. **Do not overwrite their campaign automatically**. A clean pre-GM export is a recovery point.

**Root cause and invariant:** GM's `countryStatPatches` channel is for **direct, intentional administrative baseline corrections**, not economic simulation. Existing validation enforced component-backed baseline availability and percentage breakdown totals but did not restrict the channel by GM operation mode, so a valid-shaped but unsupported AI-authored absolute GDP became canonical. Separately, narrative event `impacts.polityChanges[].stats` can carry clock-owned numeric absolutes and is another bypass in the same authority class. This is an engine authority / scope bug, **not a mere prompt issue, GDP clock anomaly or evidence that the requested crisis increases GDP**.

**Narrow native fix:** `validateGameMasterStatsAuthority` in `src/Game/AI/gameMasterValidation.js` makes `countryStatPatches` **Direct mode only**; both `exact-event` and `world-intervention` must write economic consequences as dated/bounded `polityChanges[].statsEffects` (or remain explicitly unquantified), never by changing baseline absolute GDP, population or other clock-owned readings in an existing polity's `polityChanges[].stats` update. New polity lifecycle seeding and nonnumeric currency metadata are not indiscriminately blocked. Hooked in the shared `validateGameMasterPreviewPayload` called at both **Preview and revalidated Apply**. The GM instruction aligns with this native invariant, while the Direct administrative correction channel is preserved. No numeric validation, annual effects, economic clock, or historical data constraints were relaxed.

**Regression proof:** `src/Game/AI/gameMasterValidation.test.js` includes the 1912 response-shaped 515B GDP case, checks rejection in World Intervention and Exact Event, preserves Direct corrections, approves typed dated growth effects, rejects absolute macro/population bypasses through polityChanges, retains valid nonnumeric metadata, and asserts the preflight/Apply shared routing. Focused native GM test suite **30/30 passed, 0 failed**. Final full suite: `npm.cmd test`, exit 0, **8,209 total, 8,206 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped, 2 TODO**. Focused ESLint: exit 0 (existing 9 nonfatal warnings in unchanged parts of `gameplay.js`). Production desktop `npm.cmd run build`: exit 0. Production web `npm.cmd run build:web`: exit 0, six map assets verified (66.1 MB). Live real-provider reapplication after the fix is intentionally deferred to the user's protected replay from the pre-GM export; tests/builds do not constitute that observed live acceptance. All evidence logs are under `%LOCALAPPDATA%/OpenHistoria/LiveDiagnostics/`. This follows the previous Ruhr direct-receipt coverage fix and is one focused post-freeze release blocker, not a Statistics V2 rescope.

## 2026-10-28 - POST-FREEZE LIVE PLAYTEST: SAME-EVENT ECONOMIC COVERAGE RECEIPT

**Scope:** One confirmed bug from the user's real 1912 Gemini `jumpForward` playtest, not a reopening of the Statistics V2 feature scope. A 90-day skip from 1912-01-01 to 1912-03-31 generated a German Ruhr coal-miners strike on 1912-02-08. The accepted event `event-ai-r0002-19120208-005` was `economicAccounting.status = applied`, with a verified German `production/direct` exposure, a native `annualRealGrowthDelta: -0.2` for 30 days, a matching dated `world.statsEventReceipts` process receipt, and a retained `countryStats['German Empire'].continuity.economicInfluences` record. The actual national macro clock settled normally and displayed approximately EUR 168.9B GDP at 1912-03-31. Yet both the world audit and Country Economy panel reported **one documented disruption with no accepted numeric assessment**. The exported save and player diagnostic logs establish the contradiction; the 1912 save itself is not the patch.

**Root cause:** `src/runtime/statsEconomicCoverage.js` previously counted only later `assessesUnquantifiedEventId` follow-up assessments as resolved. It ignored legitimate directly applied, *same-event* quantified GDP/growth effects, even when a matching native receipt proved their application. The read-only coverage audit and country panel share this reader. This was a false-positive completeness warning, **not** a missing economic simulation effect or a reason to change the economic clock.

**Narrow correction:** The coverage reader also recognizes an already-applied original national exposure only if the original event declares `applied`, it has one unique matching normalized accepted Stats receipt for the **same event ID, original event date and canonical polity**, and the original event contains the same polity's GDP stock change or a finite dated growth influence matching the receipt's exact rate, cause and duration. Debt-only, stability, population, model-prose-only, other-country, wrong-date, mismatched-rate and explicitly `unquantified` cases remain unresolved. Older source-linked follow-up crisis validation, receipt pruning guards, viewer audience filtering and all macro writers are unchanged. No saved game or scenario was edited.

**Observed actual save acceptance:** Running the repaired reader and global audit against the user's real 1912 working save (read-only) returned `German Empire: status none, confirmedCount 0, assessedCount 1`; global `documentedUnresolvedCount 0`, `assessedCount 1`, while correctly retaining `noNationalEvidenceCount 1` elsewhere. A new focused regression using distilled values from the user's real Ruhr event **failed before the fix** and passed afterward; 19/19 economic reader/audit focused tests passed, including bad receipts and isolated countries. Focused ESLint passed. Post-fix validation: npm.cmd test exited 0 (8,207 total, 8,204 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped, 2 TODO). Desktop npm.cmd run build exited 0. Web npm.cmd run build:web exited 0 with six verified map data files (66.1 MB). The first desktop build wrapper was interrupted by a nonfatal Babel stderr note; the second run completed and reported exit 0. The original 1912 game was not modified.

**Git custody:** Local user branch `feat/stats-v2-war-integration-2026-10-21`; preserve user's preexisting clean state and never reset, merge, push, or commit without the requested Git action. The confirmed patch is confined to the shared coverage reader, a regression test, and this canonical checkpoint. **Release remains a separate Beta reconciliation gate**; the Stats V2 implementation freeze continues.

## 2026-10-28 - CONTINUUM-1 full simulated Timeline skip / structured provider / synthesized-front event shape hotfix

**Committed baseline and custody:** The earlier 15-file native Statistics/war integration checkpoint was committed on `feat/stats-v2-war-integration-2026-10-21` as **`9edaf3943d49de4865fc8b5294e7dcc1c99654b9`**, message `feat(stats): validate dated wartime fronts and naval economic settlement`; Git reported a clean working tree immediately afterward, with no push or merge. The NEW hotfix in this section was investigated after that commit, so source and tests are modified **after** the recorded baseline; do not conflate it with code already committed. The 1912 source game/world/events remained read-only, and all test-campaign copies were isolated under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenHistoria\LiveDiagnostics` using `OH_DATA_DIR`; backed up before hotfix under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenHistoria\PatchBackups\continuum1-full-mock-front-canonical-shape-20261028`. No personal provider keys, live Gemini call, source save writes or external network access.

**Real full turn, no provider:** The exported **real** `simulateTimelineJump({days:1})` was invoked through Vite SSR, against an isolated copy of the actual 1912 campaign and an API adapter to the real desktop store. With no AI connection, the standard native application identified no available model and landed its deterministic fallback: Jan 1→Jan 2, round 1→2, event count 12→13, snapshot and genuine six-domain `writeRuntimeTurnState` save. The separate exported **real** `rollBackToSnapshot(0)` reversed it to Jan 1/round 1/12 events, original GDP and no fleets; the source game stayed unchanged. Missing stock PMTiles from the deliberately limited test adapter produced the documented fallback to the scenario's own custom region map, not a claim of stock-map parity. Evidence `continuum1-1912-full-turn-gated-sandbox-20261028\{OFFLINE_FULL_JUMP_PREFLIGHT_RESULT.json,OFFLINE_FULL_FALLBACK_UNDO_RESULT.json}`.

**Real provider router, offline fake protocol:** Configured only a sandbox-local, keyless **OpenAI-compatible** test endpoint whose `fetch` was intercepted in-memory and blocked all external requests. The real `callAI` transport sent a streamed `submit_jump_result` request. The mock deliberately returned a streaming refusal, and the production code correctly reissued a buffered request; the mock then returned a structured, schema-shaped tool answer. The real Timeline skip applied it, with `generation.source:'ai'`, an empty fallback reason, one new event, and canonical save/snapshot. Real Undo restored the source state. This tests **routing/response validation and production application** but is NOT a genuine model-generated turn. Evidence `continuum1-1912-mock-provider-full-turn-20261028\{MOCK_PROVIDER_FULL_JUMP_RESULT.json,MOCK_PROVIDER_GAMEPLAY_UNDO_RESULT.json}`.

**60-day full coordinator exposed real native-event bug:** A fresh copied 1912 campaign was given **four artificial Italian blockading squadrons** on open-water stations adjoining the actual Ottoman-controlled coast (test orders, NOT historical canon). The real `simulateTimelineJump({days:60})` accepted a mock structured diplomatic event, ran the authentic native frontline/naval engine, then crashed **before commit** on `TypeError: event.impacts.createdChats is not iterable` in `applySimulationResult` while iterating `freshEvents`. Root cause: `publishDatedFrontControlEvents` created a valid date-true native territorial event containing only `impacts.regionControlOps`. While the canonical territory reducer normalizes a separate copy, subsequent application subsystems iterate other impact-family arrays on the *original event object*. Thus synthetic events needed the **same complete canonical impact-family shape as normal generated events**. The original isolated campaign remained at Jan 1/round 1 without a turn commit. **Track and custody:** Installed on the live modern Beta feature branch `feat/stats-v2-war-integration-2026-10-21`, HEAD `6f422d68521c4c2f2022688a78e747993def6b27`. **Precise architectural seam:** `runTurnFronts` / `attachFrontFlips` currently parks native regional control changes on the nearest narrative event, which might fall days away from the actual capture (or not exist at all). The production canonical event reducer and Statistics dated clock both read **accepted event dates**, so the earlier source guard correctly refused an unmatched 90-day 1912 front replay. The new `publishDatedFrontControlEvents`, called **after** resolved native fronts but **before** canonical event/Stats application, removes only provisional `by:'front'` national control ops and republishes each on its **actual front engine day**. It reuses a same-day public event ONLY when that precise engine operation already rode on it; otherwise it creates one bounded, explicitly `source:'front'` canonical event per affected date with a deterministic round+day ID. Model narrative and authored operations retain their relative sequence; public controls do not attach to private reports or unrelated articles. An invalid, duplicated, out-of-window flip, nonchronological canonical event list, stable-ID collision or exceeded bound refuses publication instead of silently losing territory. Unsupported fantasy calendars continue through existing non-native date semantics. Group-control flips are deliberately unchanged and remain a separate watch item.

The generated 90-day native fronts recorded region 370 Italian→Ottoman control on **Jan 11** and region 367 Italian→Ottoman on **Feb 12**, both absent from the AI event list and accompanied by the corresponding verified dated naval control chronology. The new publisher created exactly two native front events and the existing `applyEventImpactsToWorld` wrote both canonical regional controllers, while `planNativeStatsLiveTurn` accepted the full national writer and provided **Jan 31, Feb 29, Mar 31** history for both Italy and Ottoman. Italy GDP 36,140,000,000→36,631,896,776; Ottoman 25,445,000,000→25,791,661,911 in the detached, no-new-AI-events pass. This is simulated engine behavior, **not approved campaign history**, because the detached harness did not also publish fleets, manpower, personnel and full gameplay state. The original saved `game.json`, `world.json`, and `storage/events.json` hashes remained unchanged. Two independent complete detached reports were byte-identical with SHA-256 `378A7AF987BA674E6768C2BFD8A6663DFF12AEF713CD92BEAA68E2126F25F023`; they are under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenHistoria\LiveDiagnostics\continuum1-dated-front-90day-{A,B}-20261027.txt`. No paid Gemini request was necessary.

**Focused validation and broader gates:** New tests check date-true native synthetic events, idempotency, private/public separation, unrelated narrative rejection, historical-ID/date/mutation guards and existing national Stats+territory canonical fold. The source-level front integration test was strengthened to check the new ordering and input/identity provenance. This could yield naval/occupation history inconsistent with the published world. This is a source-level fail-open reproduction, NOT evidence of a completed full production time skip or of a historical outcome in the actual 1912 campaign.

**Narrow engine repair:** Wartime positive/complete-zero naval evidence now requires each front-generated control flip to have exactly one accepted event-carried `op=control, by=front` mutation with matching **actual calendar day, region ID, source and destination**. Missing/unattached, mismatched, duplicate, wrong-operation or different-day receipts refuse the full native cutover. All preceding naval/territorial validation remains intact; no made-up event, changed GDP estimate or new writer was added. A legitimate exact-dated front capture still passes. The earlier 90-day no-event case now fails closed; no undocumented fallback can publish those two flips as if applied. This also exposes a follow-up: `attachFrontFlips` may attach a generated flip to the closest narrative event on a **different** date. Native full wartime settlement must not use such imprecise carriers until the authoritative dated front/canonical event seam is reconciled, including empty-event periods. Do not remove the new guard to make the 90-day test green.

## 2026-10-27 - CONTINUUM-9 live Gemini correction and fiscal-admission parity

**Live provider state (current source, NOT installed Beta):** After the two earlier provider-503 corrective responses, Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite successfully returned **three additional HTTP 200** responses against the current-source standalone Opossum diagnostic on 2026-10-27. All requests used explicitly authorized, immediately unlinked single-use credential files, separate output directories and the original read-only 2127 Opossum save. No new production provider transport was invented. (1) A correction to the existing Dyke-and-Rook League response passed jump schema after adding a bounded `economicShockEstimate` but native settlement rejected its **mixed estimated growth influence + 0.5%-GDP debt-financed outlay**, an existing fail-closed invariant. (2) A newly generated focused response proposed a national -1.2pp growth influence and 0.005%-GDP borrowing **without `debtFinancedOutlayCause`**, triggering another native rejection. (3) A corrective response to that second output **passed native settlement** for one -1.2pp annualized, 30-day crisis influence in Dyke-and-Rook League, with source storyline `global-financial-collapse-2127`, direct credit-exposure evidence and no unsupported borrowing. A correction to the first focused output had already **passed native settlement** for a -1.2pp, 45-day influence. Both are independently generated provider corrections, not synthetic edits, but **neither included `assessesUnquantifiedEventId`**; they generated no accepted historical-closure receipt. Real accepted native negative influences: **two independent one-week replay outputs** (one in each separate test, not stacked into one saved campaign). Historical crisis assessment closure: **zero**. All 47 nations still displayed positive weekly GDP changes under these single-country drags; no world-wide collapse coefficient was manufactured.

**New verified contract gap and narrow fix:** `planStatsEventEffect` already prohibits an estimated crisis growth influence mixed with independently quantified debt/stock/population/strategic effects and requires every debt-financed outlay to pair with `debtFinancedOutlayCause`. `validateGeneratedStatsEconomicAccounting` now catches both rules *before* native application and emits source/country-localized actionable errors, rather than letting a tool answer appear schema-valid and die late in Stats settlement. No native bounds were relaxed. More importantly, testing the archived fiscal-error response revealed that generic last-chance schema salvage could remove an entire `polityChanges` item from an `economicAccounting.status="applied"` event when a nested required field was missing. `protectedJumpEconomicPaths` now protects the **whole affected polity-change item as well as the `statsEffects` object** for applied economic changes and explicit historical assessments, so the model must repair the change or the turn fails closed. Both production `runJsonTask` and offline provider replay use this shared helper. Harmless optional fields on unrelated events remain salvageable. Targeted regression tests cover mutually exclusive effects, missing fiscal causes, prevention of silent list-item removal and preserved original provider errors.

**Evidence:** Provider captures under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenHistoria\LiveDiagnostics\opossum-focused-credit-repair-20261027-2022`, `opossum-credit-focused-fresh-20261027-2030`, and `opossum-fresh-credit-corrected-20261027-2035`; additional archived no-network replays at `opossum-credit-repair-incompatible-replay-20261027-2024` and `opossum-fresh-borrow-cause-preflight-guarded-20261027`. All are detached reports, not canonical saves. The accepted influence did not close the August 22 original event precisely because the generated accepted reply omitted its stable ID, not because the native writer dropped that ID. Do not infer the link or silently mark it assessed. All outputs stay private and local; rotate the shared provider key after live testing.

## 2026-10-27 - CONTINUUM-9 Statistics admission parity with validated retained assessments

**Confirmed source-contract mismatch:** After the preceding source candidate, both the Country Panel coverage reader and the AI review briefing use `indexRetainedEconomicAssessments` to ignore invalid and noncausal historical assessment claims. However, the canonical `planStatsEventEffect` writer still used an older unrestricted `historicalEvents.some` check, treating **any** prior `statsEffects.assessesUnquantifiedEventId` string for the same polity as permanent evidence that the original crisis was already assessed. A malformed event or one with a different source, future date or duplicate event identity could therefore cause AI and the Country Panel to say **unassessed**, while the writer rejected an otherwise valid bounded source-linked national follow-up.

**Minimal fix:** `src/runtime/statsEventEffects.js` now uses the same **read-only validated retained assessment index** that backs both other consumers. It also verifies the previous accepted estimate's canonical source link to the original crisis and strictly later date before barring the first valid assessment. The pre-existing native receipt check, original-event causal validation, one-estimate-per-active-source limit, finite shock bounds, canonical writers and national history guards remain unchanged. This repair does not itself create GDP losses or require new AI fields.

## 2026-10-27 - CONTINUUM-9 retained assessment evidence after receipt pruning (post-commit source candidate)

**Concrete source-level invariant:** `normalizeStatsEventReceipts` deliberately retains at most **1,200** accepted numerical receipts. `src/runtime/statsEconomicCoverage.js` already recognized valid accepted national economic assessment events from retained history when the receipt was gone. However, the *AI's unresolved-crisis briefing* in `statsUnquantifiedAssessmentContext.js` consulted **only** receipt-backed assessments. That mismatch could reintroduce a previously assessed country as a new open task, even while the Country Panel correctly reported it assessed, and could tempt a duplicate country/original event assessment. The baseline Opossum replay has zero accepted national loss assessments, so this is an adjacent proven source-contract gap, **not** a claim it caused that exact turn's failure.

**Narrow fix:** Extracted the existing retained-acceptance scan into a shared, strictly validated `indexRetainedEconomicAssessments` in `statsEconomicCoverage.js`, without changing Country Panel viewer/audience or receipt admission behavior. The AI briefing now combines durable validated accepted-event assessments with native receipts, resolves country aliases, deduplicates country/source pairs, requires exact original historical event date and canonical source-storyline linkage, and preserves privacy at its host-internal boundary. Ill-formed negative effects, wrong sources, same-day or future claims and duplicate event IDs fail closed. The bounded field was renamed from `receiptBackedAssessedPolities` to **`acceptedAssessedPolities`**, explicitly describing either accepted-event or receipt evidence. No second ledger, new AI call, prompt-budget increase, invented macro loss, writeback or schema relaxation.

**Regression:** `statsUnquantifiedAssessmentContext.test.js` tests country-specific retained closure after zero receipts and rejects invalid/future/duplicate/wrong-source evidence; the existing native assessment test was updated for the clarified bound field. Existing coverage-reader regression still covers viewer privacy and retained historical closure. The Country Panel and AI now share one retained-event assessment proof.

**Validation (executed 2026-10-27):** Focused economic briefing/coverage/native Statistics suite passed **46/46** after the retained-assessment refactor. The final `npm.cmd test` run passed **8,169**, failed **0**, skipped **1**, with **2 TODO** (**8,172 total; exit 0**). Targeted ESLint of the four changed JS/test files exited **0**, and `git diff --check` exited **0**. `npm.cmd run build` and `npm.cmd run build:web` both exited **0**, with six web map assets verified (66.1 MB). ## 2026-10-27 - CONTINUUM-9 bounded crisis-review fairness after the economic coverage checkpoint

**Committed starting point:** The prior Statistics V2, multiplayer and time-skip event-preservation checkpoint was committed locally as `9752d6664dd98bf360c3e074dd9069bcc6a70b4c` on `feat/stats-v2-war-integration-2026-10-21` with 16 intended paths; the working tree was clean after that commit. **Evidence and defect:** The uploaded Opossum replay on 2127-11-02 holds **11** existing `economicAccounting.status=unquantified` declarations from previous turns, but `buildUnquantifiedEconomicAssessmentContext` exposes a standard maximum of **8**. Its static most-recent-first scheduling repeatedly chose the same recent records and one old record, leaving intervening unresolved events out of every future jump's evidence context. That is deterministic starvation, not a numeric GDP defect. The saved legacy entries lack `economicAccounting.exposures`, so they must remain tentative unless subsequent evidence supports country-specific confirmation.

**Minimal native repair:** `src/runtime/statsUnquantifiedAssessmentContext.js` retains the existing hard bound (default eight, maximum twelve) and direct source-event IDs, prioritizes two recent actionable entries and one oldest case when the limit allows, and fills remaining slots with a deterministic rotating window keyed to the **canonical in-game week** through `gameDateDayNumber`. Across a stable backlog, all unscheduled cases enter the review context within bounded future weekly windows; no extra AI calls, narrative keyword guesses, new saved queue or inferred GDP losses. The selection is stable for the same game date/history after save/reload and across multiplayer peers.

**Regression:** `src/runtime/statsUnquantifiedAssessmentContext.test.js` exercises an **11-event Opossum-shaped backlog** over four consecutive seven-day skips: all 11 distinct IDs appear in a bounded eight-per-turn briefing, repeated reads on the same date match byte-for-byte, and older existing tests preserve quote/bound/source guards. This is proof of coverage scheduling, **not** proof that a live provider accepts measured financial consequences, that coverage is actually assessed, or that all 47 positive GDP trends are justified.

## 2026-10-27 - CONTINUUM-9 time-skip event-preservation integrity gate (source candidate)

**Observed live failure:** The October 27 Opossum replay for **2127-10-26 to 2127-11-02** (Gemini `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`) produced an Economy event, `Steppe Relay Khanate Formalizes Silver-Standard Barter Tariffs`, with `economicAccounting.status=applied` but no measurable `polityChanges[].statsEffects`. With Save AI requests enabled, the first answer was on the schema salvager's last-chance path. Salvage deleted malformed `economicAccounting`, then the **entire event**, and accepted three surviving events. Both the accepted `summary` and folded `history` still declared that tariffs were finalized. This is a provider-output/schema-salvage **narrative vs canonical timeline contradiction**, not a GDP arithmetic failure. Native Statistics settlement was `native-full` (47 initialized country sheets) and the coverage audit found **0 documented structured exposures, 60 tentative crisis-associated country/event pairs, 0 assessed**; these are not 60 verified losses.

**Narrow native repair:** `src/Game/AI/schemaSalvage.js` now accepts optional `protectedArrayItems`: disallow truncating a protected top-level array or removing its **direct** element, without blocking salvage of optional bad properties and nested array items. `src/Game/AI/gameplay.js` supplies `["$.events"]` for **`jumpForward` and `autoJumpForward` only**. If an event is irreparably malformed and its removal would leave independent summary/folded history or ledger claims asserting a nonexistent event, the candidate fails schema validation and takes the existing corrective retry/fallback path rather than being deceptively marked accepted. Harmless malformed tags and inner operations can still be dropped with receipts. Other task types (including pregame bootstrap) retain existing salvage policy. This contract does not invent `statsEffects`, alter GDP, or silently downgrade `applied` to `none`.

**Regression:** `src/Game/AI/schemaSalvage.test.js` adds an Opossum-shaped real-schema test with unsupported `applied` and contradictory summary/history, a missing-title direct-event guard, valid optional-tag salvage, protected array truncation, and source-wiring check. These are schema-boundary tests, not evidence of provider willingness to repair a second attempt.

**Explicit limits:** Does not reconcile arbitrary summaries with events later withheld by the Timeline Curator, resolve unsupported economic models, prove negative GDP effects, or retroactively repair saved folded history. Further summary/curation divergence needs independent reproducible evidence and a separate patch. Economic-causality acceptance remains open; no paid provider replay was performed for this fix.

**Validation (executed 2026-10-27):** Focused jump-schema/salvage, prompt routing, national coverage, receipts, multiplayer two-client, and update-notice checks **93/93 pass**. ## 2026-10-25 - CONTINUUM-9 native cross-turn economic coverage accountability (source candidate)

**Authority and scope:** Introduced `src/runtime/statsEconomicCoverageAudit.js` as a **read-only**, bounded and deterministic account of every existing initialized national Statistics sheet's outstanding economic coverage. It calls the existing `createCountryEconomicCoverageReader` **once per audit**, explicitly opting into **host-only canonical private evidence** while normal per-viewer Country Panel reads and multiplayer projections retain their original audience filters. It reuses indexed event/storyline/receipt evidence rather than inventing a second GDP ledger or unbounded national history. Each country/event pair is classified as **`documented-unresolved`** (named national credit/trade/production/logistics/labor evidence with no validated later country-specific assessment), **`no-national-evidence`** (active crisis association but NO documented country-specific exposure, explicitly NOT a completed review or an economic loss), or **assessed** (a valid later source-linked country-specific numerical estimate). Exact aggregate counts are computed before capped eight-row diagnostic samples. Invalid date, history or Statistics ownership produces `unavailable`, never a falsely passing completion. No existing save formats or public scenario data are changed.

**Generic failure resolved:** A November 2, 2127 Opossum-shaped regression preserves Ferric Crownlands and Dyke-and-Rook League as **two** documented unresolved national credit disruptions after Steppe Relay Khanate and Ringtail Civic Republic receive unrelated **positive** dated economic influences. Other storyline participants remain **unverified** absent their own evidence; the audit neither marks them as genuinely affected nor calculates negative GDP. A later **validated** Ferric assessment closes **only Ferric** and retains Dyke-and-Rook as outstanding. This is not a new policy requiring any particular quantitative loss. Assessing a prior event remains optional until country-level evidence and simulation assumptions support it.

**Post-settlement enforcement and reporting:** `applySimulationResult` now invokes `buildNationalEconomicCoverageAudit({ world: nextWorld, events: nextEvents, date: nextGame.gameDate })` after native event effects, national continuity and storyline settlement and **before** publishing canonical documents. Incomplete coverage appears in the turn's detailed `National economic coverage audit.` diagnostic, with a bounded sample. When either **documented unresolved** exposure or **unverified legacy crisis association** remains and a real AI jump completed, the existing application receipt records a single bounded `short` carry-forward note with **separate counts**. This includes the original Opossum 2127 saved events that lacked modern structured `economicAccounting.exposures` fields; no storyline member is thereby declared to have suffered a verified national GDP loss. A later request cannot implicitly treat a successful turn or another country's recovery as economic completion. The next-jump causal briefing retains original IDs through its independent bounded context; no new request, provider-specific prompt or bespoke save-state writer is added. This audit is observational: it **does not reject a legitimate turn** merely because measurements are unavailable.

**Late receipt persistence seam:** Canonical `simulationHistory[0].receipt` is already normalized when `applyEventImpactsToWorld` creates the first post-event world. A later audit `noteReceipt` on the original collector alone would not reach the saved newest record. `src/runtime/applicationReceipt.js` now owns `withLatestApplicationReceipt(world, receipt)`, which reattaches only the final bounded normalized receipt to the newest turn without touching older records or GDP. `applySimulationResult` uses this after the audit appends its carry-forward note. A real reference-separation test and jump source-wiring test guard this exact persistence seam. The existing receipt schema and retained note caps are unchanged.

**Receipt-pruning integrity:** The older economic-coverage reader treated any retained later `assessesUnquantifiedEventId` and `sourceStorylineId` pair as sufficient evidence of assessment closure after accepted receipts aged out, even when no quantified effect existed. The closure now requires the retained effect to contain an explicitly marked `simulation-estimate`, valid channel/exposure/evidence, finite negative `annualRealGrowthDelta` within the supported direct/dependent bounds, 1–90-day duration, compatible `gdpCause` and an evidenced reason; duplicate/future/private/foreign-source guards remain. Existing three multiplayer/coverage regression fixtures were updated to carry valid accepted assessment shapes rather than relying on metadata-only fake closure. The authoritative `statsEventReceipts` writer, economic effect admission and numerical clocks are **unchanged**.

**Privacy and remaining gate:** Host-only diagnostic samples may include source IDs; the existing multiplayer projection strips host storylines, receipts, simulation-history application receipts, and internal event source metadata. No audit source IDs are added to guest-visible country views. The Opossum financial contagion provider/live replay and genuinely supportable quantitative downturn remain **unverified and release-blocking**. This patch cannot make Gemini propose a recession or synthesize a bilateral credit network. Cross-era simulations, ordinary 1912 wartime accounting and real multiplayer UI acceptance remain distinct gates.

**Final Windows validation (executed):** The focused source, receipt, coverage, multiplayer and notice tests passed **77/77** after the last legacy carry-forward change; a broader targeted bundle including native GDP effects and jump prompt routing passed **106/106** immediately before that final note-wording adjustment. The **final** `npm.cmd test` run passed **8,161**, failed **0**, skipped **1**, with **2 TODO** (**8,164 total, exit 0**). Both `npm.cmd run build` and `npm.cmd run build:web` completed with **exit 0** on the frozen implementation; the web build verified and staged six map assets (66.1 MB). Logs: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenHistoria\LiveDiagnostics\continuum9-coverage-contract-freeze-full-suite-20261025.log`, `continuum9-coverage-contract-freeze-desktop-build-20261025.log`, `continuum9-coverage-contract-freeze-web-build-20261025.log`. The canonical audit is source-integrated, but **no live Opossum provider re-run** was done. ## 2026-10-25 - Opossum November 2 diagnostics-log audit: accepted generation omitted national crisis assessment

**Evidence reviewed:** User-supplied `open-historia-log-2026-10-25T13-06-12.txt` (Open Historia detailed diagnostics export, 1,252 lines), specifically the actual recorded **2127-10-26 -> 2127-11-02** seven-day turn. This is an inspection of the *existing* model turn, not a fresh provider run or a new state mutation. The earlier saved November 2 country/receipt comparisons remain separately authoritative for persisted macro values.

**Observed exact provider/acceptance boundary:** `jumpForward` assembled a 130,219-character prompt context and included a **8,594-character `statsCausalBriefing`** in its fingerprint; the current `buildJumpLiveState` source consumes that briefing, but this log does **not** include the complete rendered system prompt and cannot certify its verbatim delivery. Gemini `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` answered on **attempt 1**, with one request; the task succeeded without a logged rejected attempt or malformed generated-effect removal. The **accepted payload** contained five events, retained **5/5** by the Timeline Curator, with `economicAccounting` outcomes **2 applied, 0 newly unquantified, 3 none**. Two real typed country-level operations were present: Steppe Relay Khanate **+0.2 percentage points annual real growth for 180 days** (trade, silver barter tariffs), and Ringtail Civic Republic **+0.1 points for 90 days** (policy, public works). Both linked to `global-financial-collapse-2127`. The accepted events contained **no negative national growth effect, no `economicShockEstimate`, and no `assessesUnquantifiedEventId`** linking prior losses. The source's previous **11 unresolved unquantified historical events were not assessed** by either effect. The accepted storyline update continued the crisis; director warnings stripped two non-material *other* storyline updates, not Statistics effects. Exact raw pre-salvage provider tool input was not retained in this text diagnostic; a silent pre-acceptance transformation cannot be excluded on this evidence alone.

**Validator contract confirmed in actual source:** `src/runtime/statsEconomicEventAccounting.js` `validateGeneratedStatsEconomicAccounting` checks each *generated event*: allowed typed Statistics writers, `applied` iff a measurable economic effect exists, well-formed `unquantified` evidence, and accounting reason. It does **not** enforce that a later turn assesses an earlier materially disrupted national economy or emits a new event addressing that historical omission. `unquantified` is explicitly permitted when a numerical estimate lacks support. Thus the actual accepted generation satisfied the existing native rules while the causal national coverage gap persisted. The native GDP clock **demonstrably processed** two positive effects; this trace does not demonstrate a negative effect rejected by the applier. The evidence establishes an **accepted-response coverage/completeness omission**. It does not prove why Gemini declined to estimate a negative effect, nor justify fabricated losses or forcing every crisis participant into a recession.

**Next engineering gate:** Preserve the validated national source-ID-linked qualitative coverage and privacy projection; inspect actual complete AI telemetry/payload when available to separate model omission from pre-acceptance transformation. Define a **general, deterministic national coverage review/completeness contract** that distinguishes (a) documented unresolved disruption, (b) evidence-backed estimated national effect, (c) explicit unsupported/no-evidence outcome without inserting arbitrary GDP changes. It must not use narrative keywords or weaken validators and should be tested on the historical Opossum replay before any provider retest. Source implementation, game save, official scenario, branch and Git state were not modified by this log audit; only this canonical analysis checkpoint was appended.

**Separate release watch:** The same diagnostics log repeatedly reports HTTP 404 for stock countries/regions/cities PMTiles and a failed cities preload while the scenario fallback geometry remains authoritative. This is **not** the cause of the national economic loss omission; investigate release asset resolution separately without expanding a Statistics hotfix.

## 2026-10-25 - Native country-specific economic coverage (source implementation, live acceptance pending)

**Root cause addressed (scope):** Opossum's global financial collapse had canonical, ongoing, `unquantified` economic events, but no country-specific national *coverage* records. The resulting GDP/unemployment numbers were plausible mathematical trajectories while omitting any documented but unmodeled national disruptions. The fix does NOT generate arbitrary GDP losses. It makes the distinction between source-confirmed national disruption and unverified storyline association first-class.

**Canonical owner:** Optional `events[].economicAccounting.exposures`, an array of at most eight unique national exposure records, each `{polity,channel,exposure,evidence}`. `polity` uses the canonical full-name key, `channel` is credit/trade/production/logistics/labor, `exposure` is direct/dependent, and `evidence` is 32-240 characters from an accepted event. This is source-event-backed *qualitative* state, not a second GDP or trade ledger. `normalizeStatsEconomicEventAccounting` preserves valid records through existing event save normalization, and jump acceptance rejects malformed, unsupported, duplicate or non-`none`-compatible records. Existing event/save structures without exposures remain compatible; absence is NOT silently interpreted as zero economic damage.

**Read model and privacy:** `src/runtime/statsEconomicCoverage.js` derives per-polity confirmed-but-unquantified exposures from the retained event records and distinguishes them from **potential-unverified** links where an active canonical storyline includes the polity but an old economic event did not establish direct national exposure. An active storyline participant is never promoted to confirmed economic exposure. The projector checks unique event/storyline IDs, dates, sourceEventIds, canonical owner identities and any private event audience. It recognizes later accepted per-country quantitative assessments from source-bound receipts or retained follow-up events after receipt pruning, without treating one country's completion as global closure; display rows are capped without losing total unresolved counts. The projection is read-only, not a state mutation or extra provider request.

**Country Panel integration:** The standard national Economy tab lazily reads canonical events, only when opened, and shows **Economic coverage incomplete** alongside the existing dashboard/legacy standard reading. Explicit source-backed disruption is displayed as documented but numerically unresolved; legacy scenario-linked events show a separate **unverified** warning and explicitly reject the inference that participation alone proves loss. Custom scenario-authored sheets remain untouched. Private-audience records are not shown to unauthorized viewers. The native deterministic economy clock and historical GDP readings are unchanged.

**Historical follow-up:** The existing bounded jump live-state briefing includes capped `documentedNationalExposures` and also retains applied events where one country's measured intervention coexists with another country's unmodeled disruption. The native `assessesUnquantifiedEventId` follow-up contract now permits such an explicitly exposed target of a partially applied event, while preserving exact original event ID, active causal storyline, dated finite estimate, deduplication and the original accepted event. The accepted output still cannot invent a macro loss just because the coverage warning exists.

**Validation/status:** Focused tests include source event normalization, strict input bounds, old-save tentative exposure, receipt and retained-event closure, private-audience filtering, mixed applied/unmodeled events, UI lazy loading and jump transport schema limits. Record exact full Windows suite/build results after executing them. Live Opossum provider acceptance and meaningful recessionary national effects are still **unverified**, so this is not a completed financial-contagion model and the release-blocking simulation-credibility debt remains open.

## 2026-10-25 - Opossum October 26-November 2 live retest: missing per-country economic coverage (open, release blocking)

**Observed on the actual development campaign:** The controlled 7-day skip advanced from **2127-10-26 (round 4)** to **2127-11-02 (round 5)**. All **47** persisted nominal GDP figures rose, all **47** unemployment readings declined, and all **47** displayed annual real GDP-growth rates remained positive. Historical `economicAccounting.status="unquantified"` events remained **11**, unchanged. The engine accepted exactly **two new Statistics receipts**: Steppe Relay Khanate **+0.2 annualized real-growth percentage points** for 180 days (trade, 2127-10-28) and Ringtail Civic Republic **+0.1 points** for 90 days (policy, 2127-11-01). Both belong to the active `global-financial-collapse-2127` storyline. Neither receipt links an earlier unresolved event via `assessesUnquantifiedEventId`, neither models a negative crisis loss, and neither represents Opossum Kingdom, Ferric Crownlands or Dyke-and-Rook League. Those three national continuity ledgers have **zero** active economic influences. Five new AI events were accepted; the two Economy events concern silver-standard barter tariffs and a public-works hiring programme. Thus the actual GDP clock **did** respond to typed positive effects, but the systemic credit-collapse impact still did **not** enter national Statistics.

**Architectural interpretation based on code and saves:** `validateGeneratedStatsEconomicAccounting` requires an `applied` label to accompany a measurable typed `statsEffects` mutation; `unquantified` is allowed without country-level exposure and does not itself affect the macro clock. The follow-up briefing now reaches `jumpLiveState` and preserves original textual evidence, but the accepted answer still supplied no crisis-loss estimates or prior-assessment closures. No raw provider request/response transcript was captured, so whether its draft omitted or lost a proposed effect remains unproven. The **confirmed native gap** is that economic-event coverage is only event-level: there is no canonical, verified list of which polities have a direct or dependent *unquantified* material disruption and thus which national growth projections omit an established crisis. Storyline participants and diplomatic `trade_economic` agreements do **not** provide numerical GDP exposure coefficients. Do not invent country GDP losses from narrative adjectives or impose one shared crisis penalty on participants.

**Required next source-driven milestone:** Design and validate a canonical, source-event-ID-bound **per-country economic exposure/coverage contract** separating (1) documented qualitative material disruption, (2) quantitatively or transparently estimated national growth influence and its assumptions, and (3) explicit unresolved/not-modeled coverage. Show unresolved national crisis exposure as a qualified Statistics reading instead of presenting the unadjusted model as fully representative. Preserve the existing numeric native clock, dated effect receipts, deduplication, storyline causal referential integrity, and prior saved games. Determine independent quantitative assumptions before any automated GDP adjustment; no prompt-only GDP decreases, invented global contagion or weakened validators. Track this as **unresolved release-blocking economic simulation credibility debt**, distinct from the verified prompt-routing hotfix.

**Evidence retained outside repository (read-only):** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenHistoria\LiveDiagnostics\nov02-state.json`, `nov02-new-events.json`, `nov02-weekly-country-comparison.csv`, `nov02-weekly-diagnostics.md`, with `after-state.json` as October 26 baseline. No campaign content or engine source was modified during this second-week analysis. This section records observed results and intended follow-up, **not** a completed engine fix.

## 2026-10-25 - Opossum October 19-26 live regression: unrendered crisis assessment briefing

**Reproduced, read-only:** On the `opossum-kingdom-v10-saved-state-session` game (The Forest Brothers), a normal weekly jump from **2127-10-19/round 3** to **2127-10-26/round 4** produced five AI timeline events plus one separate counter-intelligence event. Canonical events increased 39 to 45; economic `unquantified` declarations increased 9 to 11. **Every one of 47** nominal GDP values increased and **every one of 47** unemployment readings declined. Positive reported `gdpGrowth` readings persisted for all 47. The AI-generated events contained **zero** `polityChanges` and thus **zero** `statsEffects`. Statistics receipts remained at two; no active country economic-influence entries existed for the primary crisis countries. This establishes missing accepted causal operations, *not* rejection of an operation the AI actually returned. Nominal GDP increasing alone does not establish a real GDP gain, especially when inflation is present.

**Root-cause evidence:** The earlier native `buildUnquantifiedEconomicAssessmentContext` computed each original event's ID, date, title and generic `economicAccounting.reason`, **but omitted its description**. The July 25 Financial Collapse event explicitly said merchant transactions in **Dyke-and-Rook League** and **Ferric Crownlands** had almost stopped; the corresponding reason only said centralized monetary data was absent. That usable country-specific transmission evidence was lost from the computed review. The builder now includes a **quoted, capped 360-character excerpt of the accepted original event description** for each already-bounded row (default eight, hard maximum twelve), with tests for original fact retention, hostile text escaping, and upper bounds. No generated description is treated as numeric proof or as a universal impact on storyline participants. **The deeper prompt-routing defect:** `buildTaskContext` appended the briefing to `worldSummary`/`worldSummaryNoCity`, but the active `jumpForward` and `autoJumpForward` templates render the separately assembled `${JUMP_LIVE_STATE}` block and never consume those summary variables. Thus a valid native assessment briefing was not delivered through the actual provider prompt path. The fix now carries the bounded review as `variables.statsCausalBriefing` into `buildJumpLiveState`, immediately alongside World Director context; both manual and automatic skips use the existing `JUMP_TASK_KEYS` set. `jumpPromptCraft.test.js` protects the live-state rendering contract and `statsUnquantifiedAssessmentContext.test.js` protects bounded source evidence. No second model call, canonical state writer or output-schema relaxation was added.

**Native read-only admission probe:** Using the real saved world, the historical July 25 event ID, the active `global-financial-collapse-2127` storyline, and a hypothetical new dated Ferric Crownlands `statsEffects`, the native validation and applier **accepted** a bounded `economicShockEstimate`, wrote the expected receipt and influence **in memory only**, and left the input world unchanged. The test's example -3pp is *not* an economic judgment or a save mutation. This separates the generation omission from a proven native acceptance failure.

**Windows source validation (verified):** Dedicated jump-template/evidence tests **21/21** passed after the final prompt-route correction. Full Windows `npm.cmd test` passed **8,132**, failed **0**, skipped **1**, TODO **2** (8,135 total), exit 0. Desktop `npm.cmd run build` and web `npm.cmd run build:web` both exited **0**, including six verified/staged map assets. The final `git diff --check` exited **0** after correcting a trailing test-file blank line. None of these assertions substitute for another live AI time skip.

**Evidence records:** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenHistoria\LiveDiagnostics\before-state.json`, `after-state.json`, `weekly-country-comparison.csv`, `weekly-economy-readout.md`, and `crisis-native-admission-probe.mjs` on the authorized PC. **No live model rerun of the improved context yet.** The incomplete country-level economic impact generation remains a release watch item: do not claim the recession now behaves credibly until a subsequent controlled jump supplies validated, receipt-backed national effects and the economic trajectory responds. Current implementation is an evidence-retention and **actual jump live-state routing correction**, not bilateral contagion or guaranteed per-country assessment.

## 2026-10-25 - Unquantified economic event follow-up briefing (native read-only checkpoint)

**Root cause:** `economicAccounting.status="unquantified"` was already preserved in accepted canonical event history, but subsequent `jumpForward` requests received no bounded native inventory of those unresolved quantitative assessments. The previous output therefore became only a diagnostic note; narrative economic disruption could remain disconnected from national GDP and employment.

**Current implementation:** `src/runtime/statsUnquantifiedAssessmentContext.js` derives a **read-only** bounded assessment briefing directly from saved event declarations, rather than introducing a second persistent queue or event-to-GDP mutation. It rejects malformed or duplicate event IDs, future dates and missing/invalid declarations. Its exact-source linking checks the existing World Director storyline identity, status, `sourceEventIds`, and an origin date not later than the event. It marks active links separately from inactive/missing ones; storyline participants are explicitly **not** assumed economically affected. The briefing is escaped, capped to 12 requested rows (eight by default), prioritizes recent active-source cases while reserving one older case, and reports the total deferred count. `gameplay.js` appends it to the **existing** jump briefing beside active native influences and receipt-backed recovery hints, without a second provider call or new save fields. Any later quantitative pressure still requires a new dated accepted event, a specific per-country transmission mechanism and the existing validated `statsEffects` writer; previous dates/receipts are not rewritten.

**Windows verification (executed):** Seven focused regression cases against canonical event save/reload, exact active/inactive source status, participant uncertainty, duplicate/future/malformed IDs, deterministic bounded selection and escaped untrusted source text passed. Selected accounting, director, crisis, localization and update-notice tests **51/51 green**. Full `npm.cmd test` exited **0**: 8,127 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped, 2 TODO (8,130 total). Desktop `npm.cmd run build` and web `npm.cmd run build:web` both exited **0**, including six verified/staged web map assets. The new native read model is source-integrated but has not yet been verified through a live model/provider turn. The October 19, 2127 Opossum Kingdom campaign export was independently inspected: **9** retained `unquantified` event declarations and **all 9** still linked exactly to an active storyline using persistent event and storyline IDs. This is saved-data evidence, not proof of provider behavior.

**Remaining limitations:** No automatic GDP loss, no quantified exposure inferred from generic storyline participants/pressure, no bilateral financial contagion model, no explicit native assessment-resolution link from a later event to the original `unquantified` declaration, and no provider/live Opossum Kingdom economic acceptance yet. Only retained canonical `bundle.events` appear in the derived read model; an event withheld from retained history cannot appear. Previously accepted `unquantified` events remain untouched. Source-only green tests cannot prove that an AI supplies specific evidence or estimated impacts.
